## <term>DEMOTIC<note>Demotic refers to both the ancient Egyptian language phase and the highly cursive script that succeeded Hieratic. It was used from roughly 650 BCE to 450 CE for everyday documents and, as seen here, complex ritual texts.</note></term>
# MAGICAL PAPYRUS
### VOLUME III.
### INDICES <note>Indices are organized lists, usually at the end of a series, providing researchers with references to specific names, gods, plants, or spells mentioned across the entire work.</note>

<meta>This page marks a transition from the previously translated title page of a Russian Imperial document to a preface for a specialized Egyptological publication regarding the Demotic Magical Papyrus.</meta>

# PREFACE

*This third and final volume of the <term>Magical Papyrus<note>Specifically referring to the London-Leiden Papyrus, a significant 3rd-century CE Egyptian handbook containing spells, recipes, and rituals written in both the Demotic script and Greek.</note></term> contains a <term>Demotic Glossary<note>A specialized dictionary or word-list for the Demotic script, which was a late, cursive form of ancient Egyptian writing used for daily and literary purposes.</note></term> and various indices. Originally we had the intention of adding a chapter on the grammar of the text, as is indicated by a few references made to it in the notes of Vol. I., but various circumstances have prevented its fulfilment. We much regret the long delay in the appearance of the present volume.*

F. Ll. G. <note>Francis Llewellyn Griffith (1862–1934), a pioneering British Egyptologist and professor at Oxford University.</note>
H. T. <note>Sir Herbert Thompson (1859–1944), an English scholar who specialized in the study of the Demotic script.</note>

<summary>This preface introduces the final volume of a scholarly series on an ancient Egyptian magical manuscript, explaining that while a grammar section was originally planned, the volume now serves primarily as a glossary and index.</summary>
<keywords>Preface, Egyptology, Demotic script, Magical Papyrus, Glossary, Scholarly Publication, Griffith, Thompson</keywords>

<meta>This page follows the Table of Contents and provides the key for the phonetic system used in the following glossary, which is essential for understanding how Demotic words are organized and pronounced.</meta>

# Alphabet for the Glossary..

| | | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. | / | a | 11. | / | l |
| * 2. | { // | e | 12. | ρ, g | ḥ, ḫ <note>Phonetic symbols representing deep or guttural "h" sounds.</note> |
| | { 2), \|, <\ | ’, ’, c <note>Symbols for various glottal stops and the sound 'ayin' (a voiced pharyngeal fricative).</note> | 13. | 6, b | ẖ <note>A soft "kh" sound, similar to the German "ich."</note> |
| 3. | /// | y | 14. | q, y, <\\ | s |
| 4. | 3, ρ | w | 15. | λ, 3 | š <note>Pronounced like the English "sh."</note> |
| 5. | 4, lh | b | 16. | σ, w, v | k, g |
| 6. | µ, 2 | p | 17. | < | q |
| 7. | y | f | 18. | <, S | t |
| 8. | 3 | m | 19. | // | θ <note>A "th" sound.</note> |
| 9. | d, — | n | 20. | \|+ | z |
| 10. | / | r | | | |

\* As initial letters these two sub-groups have been kept distinct . Elsewhere no distinction has been maintained in the order of their arrangement .

Note.—Each word has been numbered for convenience of reference.

Feminine words with final <**t** <note>The letter 't' was a grammatical marker for feminine nouns in Egyptian; by this period, it was often silent in speech.</note> are entered without regard to the <**t** .

Compounds are entered immediately after the first component of the word.

All forms of the definite article will be found under µ **p**, of the demonstrative under µ **py**, and so forth.

All figures following a **V.** <note>original: "Verso". Refers to the back side of the papyrus sheet.</note> in the same line refer to the **Verso**.

Coptic words are quoted in the order <term>Sahidic<note>The dialect of the Coptic language used in southern (Upper) Egypt.</note></term> : <term>Bohairic<note>The dialect of the Coptic language from the Nile Delta (Lower Egypt).</note></term>.

"pap. Ryl." refers to a forthcoming publication of the Catalogue of the John Rylands Demotic Papyri by F.Ll. Griffith.

<summary>This page establishes the alphabetical order and transliteration system used for the Demotic glossary, including notes on grammar, dialect, and referencing conventions.</summary>
<keywords>alphabet, transliteration, phonetic key, Sahidic, Bohairic, John Rylands Papyri, Demotic grammar, Verso</keywords>

# Section A

->/ = **a**<-

1. / **a** preposition "to, on to, more than" <note>Equivalent to the Coptic letter ε.</note> Found throughout the text <note>original: "passim"</note>.

Used with an infinitive to mean "in order to" (see references 9/23, 15/14, etc.).

For use in adverbial expressions, see the entries for **mr** (no. 377), **hry** (no. 597), and **hry** (no. 671).

Used with a pronominal suffix <note>A pronoun attached to the end of a word, like "to-me" or "to-him."</note> <note>Equivalent to Coptic ερο-.</note>.

| Person/Number | Symbol | Transliteration | References |
|---------------|--------|-----------------|------------|
| 1st singular (I/me) | [demotic] | **ar-y** | 2/8, 20, 26; 5/13, 15, 33 etc. |
| 2nd singular masculine (you) | [demotic] | **ar-k** | 6/16, 31, 36; 8/4; 10/4 etc. |
| 3rd singular masculine (he/him) | [demotic] | **ar-f** | 1/18, 2/4, 4/7, 5/4, 5, 27, 28 etc. |
| 3rd singular feminine (she/her) | [demotic] | **ar-s** | 4/6, 25/9, 11, 12; Verso 7/5, 13/4 |
| 2nd plural (you all) | [demotic] | **ar-tn** | Verso 12/11 |
| 3rd plural (they/them) | [demotic] | **ar-w** | 1/11, 2/25, 4/24, 13/10, 11 etc. |

2. / **a** used phonetically in:
(1) The relative form of **stm-f** <note>A grammatical structure meaning "that he hears" or "whom he hears."</note>.
(2) As an imperative prefix <note>A letter added to the start of a word to turn it into a command.</note> with the words:
'm (no. 58), 'ny (no. 81).

[demotic] **'r** (no. 95) [demotic] **'l** (no. 109) [demotic] **wy** (no. 179) [demotic] **wy** (no. 180) [demotic] **wn** (no. 197) [demotic] **z** (no. 1060).

Also found in [demotic] **a'ny** (see **'ne**, no. 77) and in [demotic] **a-pht** (see **pht**, no. 316).

3. / see also under the entry for **s** (no. 699).

<margin>95.</margin> <image-desc>Demotic script for ir</image-desc> **ir** verb "to do" <note>Coptic: <term>eire</term> or <term>iri</term></note>

> The form <image-desc>cursive sign</image-desc> (hieroglyphic forms <image-desc>Eye of Horus/ir-glyph</image-desc> 27/10, <image-desc>hieroglyphs</image-desc> (?) 13/2) is used for the <term>stm-f</term> <note>A standard Egyptian verb conjugation used for various tenses</note> and relative forms, for the infinitive (both the absolute and construct forms), and usually for the infinitive when combined with a pronoun suffix (5/14, 24/11 etc.). However, occasionally the latter form is written as <term>iy-f</term> (<image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> 21/35, Verso 12/5), <term>iy.t-f</term> (<image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> Verso 12/5) and even <term>iy-y.t-f</term> (<image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> Verso 12/6); see entry number 28 above and compare the Coptic forms <term>aa</term> or <term>aio</term>. The imperative <note>command</note> form is <image-desc>Demotic</image-desc> <term>a.iry</term> which corresponds to the Coptic <term>ari</term> (2/26, 7/7, 8/6, Verso 14/6); though the infinitive is also sometimes used to give a command (10/32, 24/28, Verso 3/3, 18/10).

The meanings are very various and may be roughly classified as follows: —

I. used absolutely:
a/ 19/24(?). 25(3) (meaning doubtful)
->b/ **ir n** "amount to" 12/4.8, 24/24<-

II. followed by a direct object: —
> a/ "make" or "perform," for example: light 2/17; blood (meaning "to become bloody") 5/28, 27/28; magic 23/7, Verso 12/2; justice 2/26; a remedy 20/24, healing 20/23, ointment 29/5; a bandage 6/13; an invocation 16/27, 29/1; an inquiry 27/13, 28/10; to offer a prayer 10/24, 27/2; send a message 5/29, 27/24. 29; give an answer 7/7. 23, 10/8, 28/8, a command Verso 26/4, a revelation 5/14; bring a dream Verso 17/4; to pass urine <note>original Latin: <term>urinam reddere</term></note> Verso 5/4
> 
> b/ "pass (time)" 3/1, 12/13

241. <image-desc>Demotic character for b</image-desc> **b** negative particle of the <term>habitual present</term> <note>original Latin: praesens consuetudo; a verb tense indicating an action that happens regularly</note>.

> (1) followed by the **stm-f** <note>a standard Egyptian verb conjugation</note> form of **rh** <note>meaning "to know"</note> (sometimes written **ir-rh**)
> Sahidic Coptic: **MESHE=** 9/4, 21/39.41
>
> (2) followed by the **stm-f** form of **ir** (auxiliary verb <note>meaning "to do"</note>) Sahidic Coptic: **MERE=**
> 14/18, 17/10.26.28, 22/3 (compare 8/5, used as an invocation word)
>
> more usually preceded by **n e** 5/12.17, 7/9.12, 17/2.5.27.30, 19/7
> in one case (17/2) it is written phonetically <image-desc>group of Demotic characters</image-desc> **emy-s**. Sahidic Coptic: **EMES**
>
> see also no. 242

**e** to express an attendant action, "before (or, without) having

25/20, 27/15, Verso 5/3

256. <image-desc>Demotic script for "bn"</image-desc> **bn** (1) negative particle of the present and future tenses, <gloss>Coptic: **N(...AN)**, **NNE-**</gloss>
->written with auxiliary verbs and suffixes as follows:<-
1st person singular <image-desc>Demotic script snippet</image-desc> **bn-e-y** <note>I do not / I will not</note>
2nd person singular masculine <image-desc>Demotic script snippet</image-desc> **bn-k** <note>You do not / You will not</note>
3rd person singular masculine <image-desc>Demotic script snippet</image-desc> **bn-f** <note>He does not / He will not</note>
3rd person singular feminine <image-desc>Demotic script snippet</image-desc> **bn-e-s** <note>She does not / She will not</note>
->used with the present tense 6/4, with the future 6/36.37, 7/1, 8/15, Verso 5/7<-
(2) negative of the <term>imperative</term> <note>the "prohibitive" used for commands like "do not!"</note> <gloss>Akhmimic Coptic: **MN**</gloss> (*referenced in Stern, Journal of Egyptian Language 86.133; Steindorff, Apocalypse of Elias, p. 43. n.*) 20/12, 21/23

->*compare entry no. 163*<-

## 3 = m

338. <image-desc>Demotic character resembling a large handwritten numeral 3.</image-desc> **m** preposition. "in, from, as" <note>original Coptic: **n** (found throughout the text)</note>
    <image-desc>Vertical stroke and hook.</image-desc> **Hieroglyphic form** 11/11-14. 17, 23/30, V.22/6
    <image-desc>Sign sequence resembling "p3 3".</image-desc> **mw** Apparently this may represent a simple "**m**" from " 11/3, 11/15 (*mw-hn* "in")
    13/3 ("because"(?)). Perhaps it equals **m wε** <note>meaning "in one"</note> in 1/2 (compare **mw = na** in **p-n-astor** 2/17).
    It varies with **m'** (<image-desc>Sign resembling an L crossed by a diagonal stroke.</image-desc> below) in **ryz mw ryz** 2/16, **lot mw lot** 18/13,
    **lil mw lil** V.12/3.
    **mw-ky** seems to be a compound for "like" <note>a comparative term</note> 3/17, 5/14
    <image-desc>Sign resembling an L with a crossbar and a dot above.</image-desc> **Hieroglyphic form** (<image-desc>Drawing of an owl hieroglyph.</image-desc>) **m'** "like" 10/27, 27/5

     b) **my ir-f stm** "Let him do a hearing" <note>this is an alternative grammatical construction for "let him hear"</note> (<gloss>Let me <note>original Greek gloss: Μαρι (Mari) = Demotic **my ir-y**</note></gloss> 7/34, 8/2; <note>original Coptic: Μαρε- (Mare-) etc.</note>) 2/26, 3/2, 3/4, 8/5, 9/22, 9/27, 9/28, 9/30, 10/1, 10/3, 13/28, 14/14, 14/15, 16/21, 16/22, 18/32; Volume 12/8, 13/2.

364. <image-desc>Demotic word</image-desc> **<u>mn p nfr a hr-y</u>** plant-name. "None is better than I" <note>A descriptive name for a specific plant, likely reflecting its medicinal or magical value.</note> Citation: Volume 2, line 3.

365. <image-desc>Demotic word</image-desc> **<u>mn</u>** feminine noun. "a person or thing unnamed, anyone, N. (so-and-so)." <note>Used as a placeholder in legal or magical texts when a specific name is to be inserted. Equivalent to the Coptic word: ⲛⲓⲙ (nim), meaning "who" or "anyone."</note> Citations: 13/5.7, 15/17.18.19.29.30, Volume 17/7, 19/6; used with another substantive in apposition <note>Meaning it is placed next to another noun to describe it.</note> 5/22.33, 8/14, 11/17, Volume 9/7, 18/4, 24/9.12.
<gloss>///</gloss> the same. 15/15, 26/17.
used frequently in the phrase **<u>mn a. ms mw</u>** "So-and-so born of So-and-so" <note>A standard genealogical formula. Original Coptic equivalent: ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲙⲉⲥⲓⲉ ⲛⲓⲙ (nim emesie nim).</note> Referenced in: *Journal of Egyptian Language and Antiquity* (A.Z.) 1883, page 100. Citations: 11/9, 13/5.7, 15/15.16, 19/38, 20/26, 21/24, 25/36, Volume 12/8, 13/1, 16/3.4, 20/3, 33/7.8.
↑ Greek form of the same <note>Refers to the Greek practice of using "NN" or similar placeholders in papyri.</note> (cf. A.Z. 1895, pages 132 and 134) 21/21.24.26.27.32.37.39, Volume 10/10.

# h, ḥ

<image-desc>Demotic characters for lk</image-desc> **lk** see entry no. 528

521. <image-desc>Demotic characters for lby</image-desc> **lby** a verb meaning "to rave" or "to be mad" <gloss>Coptic: *libe* or *libi* <note>original: λIBE : λIBI</note></gloss> Found in various manuscript columns including 13/17, 13/19, 21/37, and Verso 12/9.

<image-desc>Demotic variant for lby</image-desc> **lby** Additional instances at 15/19, 21/39, and Verso 16/4.

Compare with *Cipher list no. 34* <note>The "Cipher list" refers to a specialized set of signs used to encode secret names or ingredients in magical papyri.</note>

522. <image-desc>Demotic characters for lwps</image-desc> **lwps** a feminine noun meaning "vessel" or "dish" <gloss>Derived from the Greek *lopas* <note>original: λοπας</note> (see also Crum, Coptic Ostraca No. 459)</gloss> Found in Verso 7/4. <note>An ostracon is a piece of pottery used as a writing surface.</note>

It is also used reflexively <note>a reflexive verb is one where the subject and object are the same, such as "to heal oneself"</note> at 17/18 and Verso 33/5.

531. <image-desc>Demotic character for ḥ</image-desc> **ḥ** a noun used with pronominal suffixes <note>word endings like "my," "your," or "his"</note> to mean "self" or "also" <gloss>Coptic: *hoo=* or *ho=* <note>original: ϩωω= : ϩω=</note></gloss> Found frequently, including 5/32, 6/10, 7/20, 10/15, and 29/30.

Various meanings include 21/11 ("tip of the feet"), 29/18 ("from its beginning"), and Verso 13/7 ("first-fruits of oil").

### h, ḥ

->Used as an adverb:—<-

> with pronominal suffix = "before" (in a spatial sense) <note>Found at locations 2/15, 2/25, 3/3, 5/19, 7/14, 9/19, 9/33, and 14/25.</note>
> **n h.t-f** = "from before him" <note>Found at 2/4.</note>
> **hr t h.t** = "in front" <note>Found at 23/5.</note>
> **t h.t** = "before" (in a temporal sense), "previously," or "at once" <note>Found at 3/1, 16/27, and Verso 7/3.</note>
> **a t h.t** = "before" or "previously" <note>Found at 3/12, 6/11, 10/15, and 25/18.</note>
> **hr t h.t** = "before" <gloss>Sahidic Coptic: *hathe*; Bohairic Coptic: *khat-he* <note>original: ϩⲁⲑⲏ : ϧⲁⲧϩⲏ</note></gloss> <note>Found at 12/3.</note>
> **n ht** = "before" <note>Found at 3/16, 5/5, 29/5, and Verso 4/3.</note>

Used with pronominal suffix <note>This indicates the "body" of a specific person, such as "my body" or "his body."</note>:—

<image-desc>Egyptian Demotic script</image-desc> 3/12.35, 14/19, 19/4.12.23, 20/30.31, Verso 20/7, 29/4, 30/2, 33/4.9

<image-desc>Egyptian Demotic script</image-desc> 3/33, Verso 19/3

<image-desc>Egyptian Demotic script</image-desc> 13/3, 21/22.24.37

Also used as the pronominal form of the preposition **hn** <note>Meaning "in" or "within."</note>:—

**n he.t** = “in” <gloss>Coptic: *en-hēt* or *en-sēt* <note>original: ⲛϩⲏⲧ : ⲛⲥⲏⲧ</note></gloss> = 12/10, 29/22, Verso 13/3

Also **a he.t** = “to, on” 15/3, Verso 7/2.5

<summary>This page lists Demotic vocabulary beginning with the 'h' sound, including nouns for 'horse,' 'wood,' and 'flame.' It highlights the grammatical transition where the word for 'body' (he.t) functions as a component of prepositions like 'in' or 'on.'</summary>

654. **khepesh** <note>original: ḫpš</note> noun, masculine. Name of the constellation known as the Great Bear. Literally "Foreleg" or "Shoulder."
<gloss>Coptic equivalents: *shopsh* or *shobsh* <note>original: ϣⲱⲡϣ : ϣⲱⲃϣ</note></gloss> Citations: 5/1, 8/17, 21/38, Verso 18/12.

656. **kheftekh** <note>original: ḫfth</note> noun, masculine. "approach to a temple" or <term>dromos<note>A dromos is a straight, paved avenue, often flanked by sphinxes, leading to the entrance of an Egyptian temple.</note></term>. Citation: 19/6.

657. **khem** <note>original: ḫm</note>
(1) adjective. "small." 
<gloss>Sahidic Coptic: *shem* <note>original: ϣⲏⲙ</note></gloss> Citations: 21/28, Verso 3/10, 4/7, 4/19, 6/6, 21/10, Verso 1/8.

(2) noun, masculine. "a little" or "a small quantity." Citations: 12/29, 13/18, 15/1, 19/28, 20/14, 24/21, 29/28, Verso 3/8, 20/6, 32/6, 32/7, 12/30, 13/18, 15/3, 21/31, Verso 6/2, 13/6, 25/6.

(3) **khem-khem** <note>original: ḫm-ḫm (ḫm sp-sn)</note> adverb. "gradually" or "little by little." 
<gloss>Sahidic Coptic: *shemshem* <note>original: ϣⲏⲙϣⲏⲙ</note></gloss> Citation: 3/10.

658. **khem-hal** <note>original: ḫm-ḥl</note> noun, masculine. "servant" or "boy." 
<gloss>Sahidic Coptic: *shmhal* <note>original: ϣⲙϩⲁⲗ</note></gloss> Citations: 1/8, 2/23, 3/11, 3/35, 11/19, 14/19, 18/9, 18/24, 23/21, 27/34, 28/14, 29/1, 29/30, 10/14, 10/18, 2/28, 2/1, 27/15.

660. **khen** <note>original: ḫn</note> preposition. "in." 
<gloss>Coptic equivalents: *khn* or *khen* <note>original: ϧⲛ ; ϧⲉⲛ</note></gloss> Citations: 1/1, 1/4, 1/7, 2/5, 2/17, 3/31, 4/3, 5/10, 5/13, 6/15, 6/26, 7/9, etc.
Recorded in a <term>hieroglyphic<note>The formal, pictorial writing system of ancient Egypt, from which the cursive Demotic script evolved.</note></term> form: 5/27, 13/14, 20/27, 21/25, 21/32, 23/27.

Used with pronominal suffixes <note>Word endings that act as pronouns; these specific forms were lost by the time the Coptic language developed</note>:

3rd person singular masculine: **khen-f** ("in him/it") 12/28, 14/17, 15/6, 27/27.
3rd person singular feminine: **khen-s** ("in her/it") 3/21, 10/24, 27/2.

# S [ 74 ]

> [Demotic script] 1/9 (?), 21/39

976. [Demotic script] **t** (1) **infinitive** form of the verb "give, put" <note>original Coptic: † ("ti")</note> 2/8, 3/10. 21-29, 4/5-7. 23, 5/4. 6. 12. 25 etc.

> **ty** infinitive form used with a pronoun attached to the end 3/32, 5/24. 25. 28, 6/8. 9, 11/21 etc.

## List of words of Greek origin

Words within asterisks are written in cipher <note>a secret script used to hide sensitive ingredients</note> in the original. It may be observed that a considerable number of these words are in the genitive case <note>a grammatical form indicating possession or "of"</note>, being names of ingredients followed by a measure of quantity, e.g. 15, 25, 32-34, 36, 37 etc.

<page-num>105</page-num>
# The Cipher

As William Groff first demonstrated in his memoir on this papyrus called "Studies on Sorcery" <note>original: "Études sur la Sorcellerie" (Mém. de l'inst. égypt. III p. 337 sq. esp. pp. 358.370)</note>, the key to the cipher is contained in some parallel passages, where words, evidently equivalent, are written in the one case in cipher and in the other in demotic <note>Demotic is a late Egyptian cursive script used for everyday documents and some literary texts.</note>. These passages are:—

α) 10/31 = 27/9 where 4U3λIIIIr = <term>smorne</term> <note>Coptic for Myrrh</note>
and A3λA3λII-ⲣλ = <term>koukouphat</term> <note>Coptic for Hoopoe (a type of bird)</note>
and f.... = <term>moulakh</term> <note>Coptic for Salt</note>
β) 5/25 = 27/25 where 3TY = <term>balsh</term> <note>Coptic for Falcon/Hawk</note>
γ) 24/26 and 38 where III49II- = <term>n sat</term> <note>Coptic for "of the tail"</note>

These equivalents furnish the values of 13 signs, which, when applied to the groups in 24/7.10 show that these clearly represent the two substances written out in demotic just below in lines 18 and 19, namely <term>mandragora</term> <note>Mandrake root, often used in ancient magic and medicine.</note> and <term>kioros</term> <note>likely a variant of 'kyphi', a famous Egyptian incense.</note>; and so values are obtained for four more signs. Confirmation is afforded by the group in 24/9 resolving itself at once into the Greek word <term>hyoscyamus</term> <note>original: "δοοκυάμου" (henbane), a poisonous plant used in rituals.</note> etc. As a result we obtain the following list of signs with their values expressed in Coptic letters. The numbers refer to the list of words in cipher on page 109 and following.

| Sign | Coptic equivalent | Remarks |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| i | — A | = demotic <term>a</term> <note>cf. 63 and demotic glossary no 725</note> |
| ii | F A | value from 61 (= Greek A) |
| iii | F F E | The numerous instances in which this sign ends a word, as well as its constant interchange with no. IV shows that it is E rather than A, the value suggested by 28 |
| iv | Ir E | Value from 62; interchanges with III and V |
| v | T E, P(?) | the value E from 6 = demotic <term>e</term> in demotic glossary no 247; the value P(?) from 29.30 |

<summary>This page details the linguistic breakthrough used to decode a secret cipher in the papyrus by comparing encrypted words with their Demotic and Coptic equivalents. It provides a partial table of the discovered alphabet, identifying botanical and ritual ingredients like mandrake and henbane.</summary>
<keywords>cryptography, Demotic script, Coptic language, William Groff, mandrake, henbane, ancient magic, linguistics</keywords>

| XXXIII | G | e occurs only in 60; compare glosses 9/6, 29/10 |
| XXXIV | y | 4 occurs only in 60; compare glosses 9/6, 29/10 |
| XXXV | b (?) | b occurs only in 60; compare glosses 9/6, 29/10 |
| XXXVI | w | { = Demotic σ K in Demotic gloss w 9/16 <br/> { = Greek K in 17.32 and y in 83 |

As appears from the above table there may be more than one sign for a given value, so that one and the same word may be written in different ways, compare 44.88 and 89, 46 and 47 etc.

Furthermore one sign may have more than one value. Signs II and XXX are undistinguishable in form, but this is the only instance of two wholly incompatible values, unless the value π <note>Greek letter Pi</note>, occurring in two instances of one word, be accepted as free from error for No V; moreover in one or two instances No XIV approximates in form to No V.

The alternative values given under XIII and XIV are closely related, since r in the glosses frequently represents both Demotic e (7/22.25.28, 16/3 etc.) and the aspirate <note>a breathy 'h' sound</note> (evidently owing to the regular aspiration of initial v <note>Greek letter Upsilon</note> in Greek) e.g.

- Demotic ρ h in 1/15.25, 2/16, 5/11.20, 7/25 etc.
- Demotic 9 h in 7/33, 8/2.3.5.6
- Demotic 9 h in 1/10.9/13

The author notes the fluidity of the script, where different signs can represent the same sound and single signs can hold multiple values.</summary>
<keywords>paleography, phonetics, Coptic, Demotic, Greek, linguistics, transcription, glosses, aspiration</keywords>

--- Page 116 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the "secret alphabet" or cipher used in the magical papyrus, focusing on the phonetic origins and comparative examples of specific signs.</meta>

<page-num>108</page-num>

The two values **T** and **X** <note>The author is discussing the phonetic relationship between the hard 't' and the aspirated 'kh' or 'ch' sounds</note> for the number 29 (compare also numbers 20 and 21) are likewise related, since both represent Coptic words written in the old language <note>referring to Demotic or Hieroglyphic precursors to Coptic</note> with the letters ⊐ and ⊏; and Greek transcriptions reproduce the sound **X** <note>the Greek letter Chi</note> using **T** or **Theta**.

A fragment of what looks like the same cipher (a symbol ☉ with a small circle at the top right) is found in Papyrus 3229 of the Louvre Museum (see Maspero, *Some Papyri*, plate II.25), but it is incomplete owing to a hole in the page and is insufficient to form a known word.

Four of the signs—namely ⊐, ≡, |||, and ⊏—appear in another secret alphabet written on the back of column 25 of our papyrus (see the Leemans facsimile, plate XIV). These apparently hold the values **A**, **Z**, **N**, and **W**, if the signs are taken to correspond to the order and values of the Greek alphabet; these are the same values they have in this section.

Of the remaining signs, five are merely Demotic letters, though slightly modified: namely **Y**, **G**, **6**, **⊏**, and **3**. The sign ⊏ is found on a mummy ticket <note>original: "tessera"</note> (see Spiegelberg, *Proper Names*, pages 18-19) with the values **Eta** and **⊏**, derived according to Spiegelberg from—

-># Magical Names<-
->## List of divine, mythological and magical names.<-

Note. As this list is compiled mainly for the use of those who are concerned with the subject of invocations <note>The act of calling upon a deity or spirit</note>, it seemed best to give in this index the approximate transcripts of the names in Roman characters used in the translation in Volume I rather than the accurate transliteration of the original Demotic <note>A late stage of the ancient Egyptian language and script</note> forms.

1.  a 17/1.27
2.  Aa 2/10, 5/11, 7/8

4.  A-a-tieu 25/4
5.  Abartat 20/16
6.  Abeiath V.22/18
7.  Aberamenthou 23/17 <note>A common magical name for Jesus in Gnostic texts, though here used in a broader ritual context</note>
8.  Abiaho 19/39
9.  Abit 7/34
10. Abla 7/33

12. Abraham 8/8 <note>The biblical patriarch, often invoked in Greco-Egyptian magic for his perceived power</note>
13. Abrasax 13/27, 23/24, V.12/8 <note>A supreme solar deity in Gnostic systems; the letters of his name in Greek sum to 365</note>

15. Adael 7/24
